**Verified** with 68 tests in an overlay (registry against Ajv and the real pin files, the scanner against the TypeScript compiler and ripgrep, funnels, cancellation within 10 s, admission order), Rust 7 tests and the Go tests; the real-repository scan read 55,579 files and reported 2 findings, both files the launcher set deletes (apply the launcher first). Not verified: the 98 migrated TypeScript files were parsed but not type-checked as a whole, no PATH-shim gate exists for foreign-language spawns, and 4,573 declared manifest commands are counted, not enforced, until the command-form wave rewrites them.

### 🔁️ Seams: what you can swap and how

Every external dependency of the build system sits behind a registry row and one interface, so that a different implementation is a new row plus one adapter module, not an edit of neutral code. Neutral code names no backend, runtime, package manager or storage format; laws keep it that way and count what is left.

| Seam | Registry | Swap | State |
|---|---|---|---|
| Runtime | the launcher registry, the facade `⚙️runtime` | `SEMIO_RUNTIME=<name>` | live |
| Tools | the tool registry | `SEMIO_TOOL_<ID>` selects the executable | staged |
| Build backend | `🏗️build/🔌️backends/📋️registry/🔣️.json`: nx, bazel, semio | `--tool=nx\|bazel\|semio` or `SEMIO_BUILD_TOOL` | staged |
| Package manager | descriptors in `⚡️caching/🚀️bootstrap/📦️dependencies/🔌️managers` | `SEMIO_PACKAGE_MANAGER_<ECOSYSTEM>` | staged |
| Storage backend | the backend registry of the native storage layer | `SEMIO_NATIVE_STORAGE_BACKEND`, `_REQUIRE`, `_PREFER` | staged |

- **Build backend.** Neutral code reaches a backend only through `loadBuildBackendV1(id)` and the interface with `setup`, `reset`, `prepareStorage`, `cache`, `remoteCache`, `execute` and, optionally, `affectedProjects`. The registry row holds the titles in English and German, the adapter and conformance driver modules, the dependency model, the per-operation support, the claim for each of 17 capabilities and the native-snapshot protocol, so the 19 per-tool rows of the operations catalogue are gone. Neutral-literal hits (`"nx"`, `"bazel"`, closed unions) fell from 190 in 40 files to 63 in 16; the rest is classified: 40 in the pairwise comparison harnesses (`parity`, `incremental`), 19 in test fixtures, 2 in a schema and 2 single sites. `--tool=semio` is the third backend, an in-house engine with no external tool: the action key is the SHA-256 of the task identity, the contents of every selected input and the output digests of the dependencies (equal outputs cut off dependents, as in Bazel), a content-addressed store, single-flight locks across processes, a scheduler with a parallel bound, `--bail` and cancellation that kills the process tree, and a client for the shared cache server. It is fail-closed: any input it cannot interpret makes the task uncached. It refuses `run --watch=<target>` and continuous tasks, does not reproduce hermetic or `bazel test` classes, and its store is a ledger area collected by `repo cache gc --area=semio-actions`.
- **Package manager.** A manager is a JSON descriptor plus a lock reader: executable and probe, manifests, lock and configuration names, isolated environment, install recipe, argument templates per operation, installed layout and resolution profiles. The adapters are bun and npm (JavaScript), cargo, go, uv and dotnet. `SEMIO_PACKAGE_MANAGER_JAVASCRIPT=npm` changes the lock name (`package-lock.json`), the command (`npm ci ...`), the layout and the key, and nothing in the flow; generations and receipts are neutral. For bun the generation key, bootstrap key and sync arguments equal the old formulas byte for byte, so the swap itself causes no cold install. Package-manager literals in production code fell from 117 in 37 files to 25 in 12, all inside adapter-owned modules or ten declared data files that a law ties to the selected manager. Open: npm through the full JavaScript driver is unproven (the staged configuration text and the isolated layout are bun-shaped), there is no `--ecosystem` flag yet, and a real install proof for the other adapters is missing.
- **Storage backend.** The native storage facade has no `process.platform` and imports no backend: it asks the registry (`mounted-image`: macOS, a case-sensitive APFS image with a kernel quota; `directory-partition`: macOS, Linux, Windows, an unprivileged directory with an atomic exchange). The selection is a pure function of platform, explicit id, required and preferred capabilities; a child, a cleanup token or a maintenance call uses the backend recorded for its parent, never the ambient platform; a directory that holds the other backend's backing file is refused. Default selection on macOS stays `mounted-image`, so `.cache/b/*` is unchanged; selecting the directory partition on macOS needs a case-sensitive volume. 23 neutral-to-backend edges remain, pinned in a fixture that may only shrink; Linux and Windows execution is not run.
- **The adapter rule.** An external library or tool is used behind an interface of ours; no exported API names a type from outside the repository; a swap is proven by running the same conformance suite against the new row.

### 📐️ The conformance suite

One language-agnostic suite judges every build backend with the same scenarios, so that a swap cannot silently lose a capability. The command below belongs to the staged set. It is level 1 and runs in seconds to minutes; the real-repository harnesses `repo incremental`, `repo verify build-parity` and `repo concurrency` stay level 2. State: staged.

```bash
repo conformance list
repo conformance plan --scenario=<id>
repo conformance run --implementations=semio
repo conformance run --implementations=nx,bazel,semio --file=<report> --json
```

- **Workspace.** 15 stub projects (a diamond, a cut-off pair, multi-output, failing, failing test, slow, single-flight, three parallel). The same data becomes an Nx workspace, a Bazel workspace or the neutral graph of the in-house engine. Ground truth for "executed" is a marker file written by a stub task body, never the backend's own record.
- **17 capabilities in 20 scenarios**: plan, run (cold, ordered, lazy closure), cache restore (also after deleting outputs), invalidation, early cutoff, uncached tasks, nested outputs, failure isolation (a failure is never cached), test results, affected, cancellation (stops within 5 s, nothing published, the next run completes), progress, parallelism (bound and overlap), concurrent invocations, single flight, remote cache (fresh checkout, empty local cache, shared server) and run record. Each scenario cites the existing evidence it generalises (incremental scenario ids, parity sample ids, concurrency workloads).
- **Verdicts** combine the registry claim with the measurement: `pass`, `gap` (claimed missing and failing: documented), `regression` (claimed and failing), `stale-claim` (claimed missing but passing), `unobserved` (the binding cannot observe it: counted apart, never a pass) and `error`. The judge has an independent Python oracle; sensitivity tests show that a backend that forgets its cache, lies about exit codes, ignores cancellation or serialises work fails exactly the scenarios that should catch it.

| Capability | nx | bazel | semio |
|---|---|---|---|
| 18 of the 20 scenarios (plan, run, cache restore, invalidation, early cutoff, uncached, outputs, failure isolation, test results, affected, cancellation, parallelism, concurrent invocations, remote cache, run record) | pass | pass | pass |
| single flight: two simultaneous invocations execute the body once | gap, as declared (Nx processes do not coordinate) | pass | pass |
| progress: a start and an end per executed task | unobserved (Nx publishes no per-task events) | unobserved | pass |

Facts the runs established: unmodified Nx also gets early cutoff when dependents declare `dependentTasksOutputFiles`; a Bazel action tagged `local` can never use the remote cache, so a backend adapter that tags tasks `local` loses sharing; Bazel keeps its products outside the workspace, so every binding must copy them back, read-only files included. The in-house engine passes all 20 scenarios through its driver and through the real CLI (`repo conformance run --implementations=semio` and `repo --tool=semio tools setup semio`); real Nx 23.2.0 and real Bazel 8.4.2 give 19 of 20 satisfied plus the one gap or unobserved row above. Not run: the scenarios through `repo --tool=semio run` of a real repository target (the graph build waited in the capacity queue), and the level-2 harnesses with `--implementations=semio`. Procedure after the apply: `repo --tool=semio run <cheap project:target>` twice (the second must restore), then `--dry-run`, `affected` and `cache gc --area=semio-actions`.

### 🧩️ Polyglot units and early cutoff per language

A unit is the smallest thing a build re-runs: one language, one kind (library, binary, test), its sources, inputs, dependencies, pinned externals, an interface and an action. The planner and the engine are language-neutral; an adapter per language only knows how to find units and what their interface is. The model and the `repo units` command are a staged set. State: units staged, pinned toolchains live.

```bash
repo units plan --for=go
repo units run --for=python --receipts --concurrency=4
repo units emit --for=cpp
repo units exec --for=go
```

- **The key and the cutoff.** A unit's key is the SHA-256 of its declared parts, the digests of its files and, per dependency, the **interface closure** (the dependency is used for compiling or checking: `interface`), the **output closure** (it is linked or run: `implementation`) or both (`both`: a native executable compiles against a library's interface and links its implementation); `declared` orders without keying. A dependent therefore re-runs only when that closure changes: early cutoff. Closures are recomputed from the current facts on every run, hit or not. A finding changed the TypeScript design: the TypeScript engine keyed only the direct dependencies' declaration digests, so with top, mid and low, where mid re-exports low's type, an edit in low left top cached while it was checked against low's declarations through mid. The neutral model keys the transitive interface closure (a unit may report which dependencies its interface mentions, which restores the narrow cone); the staged set makes that the TypeScript default.
- **Per language**, what the interface is and what a body edit does:

| Language | Interface | Evidence and effect |
|---|---|---|
| TypeScript | declarations of the unit (closure) | wraps the TypeScript units engine; a body edit re-checks one unit |
| Rust | the `.rmeta` digest | wraps `cargo metadata`; metadata holds spans and inlinable bodies, so a body edit also moves it: cutoff only on byte-identical metadata until an API digest exists |
| Go | export data (`__.PKGDEF` without its build-id line) | own scanner equal to `go list -overlay` on 53 of 53 compared packages of the repository; a body edit leaves the interface byte-identical, a signature edit moves it |
| Python | the declared API surface (`.pyi` replaces the module; unannotated returns ignored) | own lexer equal to CPython's `tokenize` and `ast` on 120 and about 100 standard-library files; a removed name fails the importer exactly as `import` does |
| .NET | SHA-256 of the reference assembly | project parser equal to `dotnet msbuild -getItem`; a private edit rebuilds the library, leaves the reference assembly and cuts off the app |
| C and C++ | comment- and layout-normalised public headers | CMake file API; `clang -E -P -dD` agrees on 8 of 8 header pairs; a comment edit in a header cuts off |
| WIT | canonical text of the world | own parser against `wit-parser` and its printer; a comment edit in a dependency is cut off |
| TeX | the normalised `.aux` (labels, citations, toc) | executor untested: tectonic is not installed on the primary host |

- **The same units under both tools.** `repo units emit` writes Nx targets whose inputs are the unit's own files plus the `iface.json` or `out.json` of its dependencies only (so Nx cuts off by itself), and a Bazel rule `repo_unit` whose interface dependencies appear only as `iface_deps`. Real Bazel 8.4.2: a Python body edit executes exactly one action (the dependents are cut off by Bazel itself), a signature edit executes the chain, a removed name fails at the importer; a pure-Go library is compiled with `go tool compile -importcfg` against the export archives of its dependencies, so a body edit of `a` executes `a`, its test and the binary and cuts off `b`; a C++ header comment edit executes the library and the app and cuts off the other library. Go binaries, tests and cgo or assembly packages, .NET and Rust units read dependency sources and cut off only in `repo units run`, not in Bazel.
- **Speed.** The Go graph of the repository (60 packages after the canonical layout merges member directories) plans in 14 s, dominated by loading the canonical plan of 44 modules; the engine's hot no-op measures 87 to 110 ms with the file store and 31 ms in memory; the Python plan of the repository takes 26 s for 10,232 files (9,927 are ticket scripts).
- **Pinned toolchains** (live). Identity is the pins plus the host plus the declared variables that are set, never an installation path, and no spawn is needed to compute it. Go 1.26.9 is a repository tool provisioned into the tools partition (size and SHA-256 checked, one atomic rename) with `GOTOOLCHAIN=local`: nothing depends on the Go on the `PATH`, and the user's machine is not modified. Python comes from uv-managed interpreters only, in an environment per lock (`.cache/b/d/m/python/environments/<id>-<16 hex>`, shared by agents; a new lock means a new directory, no live environment is mutated). The .NET SDK is pinned by `global.json` (`rollForward: disable`) and a raw build writes below `.cache/b/p/m/dotnet/raw/<project>`. CMake 4.4.4 and Ninja 1.13.2 are checksum-pinned for six hosts. Hermetic classes for these toolchains are declared and flip to provisioned by a data edit.
- **Limits.** The Bazel rule was run on Python, Go, C++ fixtures, not on the repository; Nx targets were emitted and shape-tested, not run; Windows and Linux were not run; the adapters cover what their oracles cover (7 of the 60 Go packages sit in emoji parent directories whose import path `go list` itself refuses, so they are not compared).

### 🛰️ The resident service and `--service`

A cold `repo run` pays for loading the build library (0.7 to 2.3 s on the real closure), taking the image custody (about 1.2 s) and proving membership and the closure digest (0.2 to 0.5 s) before any tool starts. The resident service keeps that state warm, per worktree and per user, and answers a warm command in milliseconds. It is a staged set: until it is applied every command takes the one-shot path. State: staged.

```bash
repo service start
repo service status
repo run <project>:<target> --service=require
repo run <project>:<target> --service=off
repo service issue
repo service stop
```

- **Who answers.** After the launcher has found the checkout and before it chooses or provisions the runtime, a plain `run`, `test` or `run-many <targets>` (flags that cannot change a task only) goes to the service over a unix socket or a named pipe. Anything else, an absent service, a directory that is not private to the user, a missing key, an unresolved language or a retry hint falls back silently to the one-shot path and prints nothing (MCP clients read that output). `--service=auto|off|require` chooses: `auto` is the default, `off` forces the one-shot path, `require` refuses in English and German when no service answers. A busy service answers with exit code 75 and its text; a connection lost after acceptance is exit code 70.
- **What it keeps.** An event-sourced state of the member and directory map (the graph frontier), per owner file digests (only dirty files are rehashed), the environment digest, task keys and a result memo. A native file watcher (one Rust file, standard library only: FSEvents on macOS, inotify on Linux, ReadDirectoryChangesW on Windows) delivers changes; directories such as `node_modules` are excluded in the kernel, so 20,000 writes inside one deliver zero rows. After a restart the persisted FSEvents cursor replays the edits made while it was down (20 of 20 recovered, no rescan).
- **The fence is the correctness core.** Before it answers, a request writes a unique file into the fence directory and waits for its event; all earlier events on that stream are applied first, so an agent that edits and immediately runs sees its own edit. Trust is reported by `repo service status`: `exact` (events and fence), `verify` (every answer re-proved by `stat` of the closure's files, with a racy-window rehash) or `none`. It drops to `verify` when the start canary fails (a bind mount that forwards no host edits is caught this way), when a fence is missed, when the watcher dies, or permanently when the inotify watch limit is reached. After an event gap (overflow, must-scan, mount change) the scope is re-walked and remembered outputs are re-verified. The tool keeps the last word whenever the service has any doubt; the service is an accelerator on top of the tool caches, never the authority on whether a task is current.
- **Numbers** (macOS arm64, 10 cores, load 26 to 48, so absolute tails are inflated and the ratios are the reliable part; a synthetic workspace of 33,000 files and 1,500 projects): a warm no-op through the native client takes p50 11.6 ms at load 26 (p50 28.6, p90 78, p99 166 ms at load 46), the service side alone p50 8 to 12 ms, of which 11 ms are the fence; from an edit to the first tool process p50 37, p90 136 ms (fresh process without the service: 378 ms; with the measured custody added 280 to 335 ms); edit to dirty in the service p50 11.5 to 12.2 ms with FSEvents; 16 clients issue 181 to 362 requests per second with p99 59 to 219 ms and no refusal. The service costs about 120 MB resident and is ready 0.55 to 1.7 s after the start. The real CLI path end to end is projected, not measured.
- **Executing in the service.** Plain task commands run in the service only through one declared kind in the authority core: a grant (`worktree`, key digest, operations, request and concurrency limits) is created at the door only for the hidden executor entry of the service, from the private key file the service wrote (mode 0600 in a 0700 directory of the user). During a request the launches that the one-shot door computes for exactly these arguments become visible through one function; the grant never reaches a child. A law fails when a second reader of `launches` appears. Each refusal (14 codes) has an English and German text. Executors are off by default (`SEMIO_RESIDENT_EXECUTORS=n`); a command that the service may not execute runs in a pre-warmed one-shot worker (module load 0.3 to 0.5 ms instead of 0.7 to 2.3 s) or cold.
- **Tokens.** `repo service issue` mints a capability for an agent session (scopes, projects, expiry; 1 h by default, 24 h at most) and keeps the token in a private file; the record never carries it. Tokens are bearer tokens and the key file is the root of trust: any process of the same user that can read the key can mint any token, so the binding to an agent is an accident and audit control, not a security boundary.
- **Limits.** Windows keeps executors off; the Bazel path is declared through the same launches but not exercised; the real `runOwnedBuildInvocationV1` inside the held custody has not run; the service measured here is the prototype in an overlay, and the first live run decides.

### 🪪️ Agent sessions

Every agent that works in the checkout has one identity that every layer reads: its Cargo lane, its preferred Bazel slot, its test sandbox root, the owner of its leases, its share of the capacity queue and its resident-service capability. The following is a staged set; today lanes, slots and sandboxes derive identity separately (`SEMIO_CARGO_LANE`, `SEMIO_SESSION`, `SEMIO_AGENT_ID`, `SEMIO_SERVICE_TOKEN`). State: staged.

```bash
repo session open --agent=<name> --weight=2
repo session open --export
repo session status
repo session list --all
repo session close --dead
repo session close <id> --force
```

- **What a session is.** A record per user and worktree (`<registry>/<worktree key>/<id>.json`, next to the lease registry; `SEMIO_AGENT_SESSION_DIRECTORY` overrides it): the agent (`id`, `client`, `origin`), the session `id` (`a` and 10 base-36 characters of a digest of the agent id, `d` for the default session), the derived names (`resources.cargo.lane`, `resources.bazel.preference`, `resources.nx.namespace`, `resources.tests.root`), the lease owner `<agent>/<id>`, a budget weight from 1 to 16, an optional capability and up to eight liveness anchors (process id and start time). The agent comes from an explicit name, else the client's own variable (`CLAUDE_CODE_SESSION_ID`, `CODEX_THREAD_ID`), else the MCP connection, else the nearest agent-client ancestor process, else the default agent.
- **One variable travels.** Between processes only the address `<worktree key>.<id>` in `SEMIO_AGENT_SESSION` travels. `SEMIO_AGENT_ID`, `SEMIO_SESSION`, `SEMIO_CARGO_LANE` and `SEMIO_SERVICE_TOKEN` are gone and nothing reads them (a source law in two suites fails when one reappears). Every heavy `repo` command attaches before the capacity gate and puts the address into its environment; light commands (`leases`, `capacity`, `session`) do not pay for it. A read-only home never stops a build: the session then lives in memory.
- **The ancestry rule matches the executable, not the command line.** The old rule tested the whole command line of every ancestor, including Claude Code's own shell wrapper that mentions the client in a path, which would have given every Bash call its own Cargo lane and never reused a warm one. One `claude` ancestor now names the same session across all the Bash calls of a task; the walk takes one `ps` listing (75 ms at load 25) instead of one spawn per ancestor.
- **State and cleanup.** `live` (seen within 10 minutes, or the default session), `idle` (owner alive within a day, or seen within an hour), otherwise `gone`. At most once a minute a command collects gone sessions: test sandboxes are reclaimed with their images detached first, the temp root is removed, a session whose sandbox still runs is kept and the default session is never collected. `repo session close <id>` refuses a live session unless `--force`; `close --dead` collects every gone one.
- **Where it is read.** Cargo lanes and the lane metadata; Bazel slot ranking (`preference mod slots`, so concurrent agents start on different slots) and the owner in the slot state; test sandboxes `<tmp>/st/<root>/<8 hex>`; TypeScript check units; run leases and the output cache; the lease registry (`repo leases status` shows `session <id> (<agent>)`); the capacity queue. In the capacity queue the order is priority, then reserved memory per weight, then arrival, so one agent with many requests no longer keeps the others waiting; the budget is an order, not a hard cap, and a lone agent is never throttled. `repo capacity status` prints a session block with grants, reserved megabytes, waiters, weight and share.
- **MCP.** The Go server asks `repo session open --json` once per agent (or per connection: client name, client pid, own pid), caches the answer and passes only `SEMIO_AGENT_SESSION`; four tools `session_open|status|list|close` exist. The running MCP binary needs a rebuild and a reconnect after the apply.
- **Limits.** The native launcher's resident client must read the address, the session record and its token file (it still read the old variables when this was staged); deriving the agent natively without an address needs the identity rules ported to Go. The optional Nx watcher identity per agent is a separate group because detached supervisors lose the process ancestry. Not run: Windows and Linux hosts, the taxonomy gates for the new directories, a real `repo session` through the built launcher.

### 🗄️ The remote cache server

The shared result cache is an in-house server, not a script: one process serves Bazel's HTTP cache protocol and the Nx self-hosted cache API from one directory, one budget and one eviction. The own command below is a staged set; today `repo cache serve` and `repo cache remote status` do this job and are described in Caching model and Remote cache. State: staged.

```bash
repo remote-cache serve --port 6090
repo remote-cache serve --read-only --tls-certificate <cert> --tls-key <key>
repo remote-cache status
repo remote-cache gc --dry-run
```

- **A command of its own.** `repo remote-cache serve|status|gc` replaces `cache serve` and `cache remote status` (no alias); messages exist in English and German (`--lang`, `SEMIO_LANGUAGE`, locale; when none resolves both are printed); progress runs through `remote-cache:open|scan|ready|serving|probe|verify|remove`; Ctrl+C stops `serve` (exit 0) and `gc` (exit 130).
- **Protocols.** `/ac/<sha256>` and `/cas/<sha256>` with GET, HEAD and PUT and an optional `/<instance>` prefix; the CAS body is hashed while it streams and refused with 400 on a mismatch; the action cache is replaced atomically. Nx uses `/v1/cache/<hash>` (write-once: 409 on a second write, 411 without a length) in the same index, budget and eviction.
- **Action results are validated.** A strict decoder of `ActionResult` and `Tree` (wire types, UTF-8, lowercase digests, size consistency) runs on every PUT. With the default policy `complete` a PUT is refused with 400 unless the result is well formed and every blob it names, and every file of an output tree, is stored (Bazel uploads blobs first); a GET answers a miss and drops the entry once a blob has left the store. `structure` and `off` are selectable.
- **Atomic and concurrent.** Temporary file plus rename or link in a per-process temp directory (dead processes' directories are swept), a byte reservation, one server per directory (a lock; a second start fails with a clear text), a separate collector lock. Two real defects of the earlier store were found by the new tests and fixed: byte accounting drifted when concurrent writers replaced the same key, and when a read raced a replace.
- **Budget and eviction.** One pure plan (dangling entries first, then idle age, then least recently used down to 90 percent of the bound, deterministic ties) is used by the server and by `remote-cache gc`, which also works while a server runs. The default directory moved from `.cache/remote-cache` (outside every budget) to the ledger area `remote-cache` (repository root, 3 GiB); the server bound is 2.5 GiB; `repo cache gc` delegates the area to the same collector.
- **Access.** Bearer read and write tokens (constant-time compare, 401 as plain text, 403 for a read token that writes); `--read-only` or `SEMIO_REMOTE_CACHE_SERVER_READ_ONLY` refuses every write; `--tls-certificate` and `--tls-key` serve HTTPS; a non-loopback listener needs a token. The client side is unchanged: the contract is rendered for Bazel (`--remote_cache`, `--[no]remote_upload_local_results`, a credential helper) and for Nx (environment), and `remote-cache status` prints the contract, both renderings, one probe per protocol, the server's own `/status` and the local store. The in-house `--tool=semio` backend writes `/v1/cache/semio-<key>` with the same write-once rule.
- **Measured.** Real Bazel 8.4.2: a cold build uploads, a clean rebuild gives 2 remote hits, and a copy of the workspace in another directory with a second output base builds with remote cache hits only; anonymous access gets 401 and a read-only client pushes nothing. Real Nx 23.2.0: 0 of 2, then 2 of 2 (100 percent) after deleting the local cache; a wrong token aborts the run. Indicative throughput of one process at load 40: 4.6k PUT per second and 5.6k GET per second of 4 KiB, 23k misses per second. Three Python processes with 24 threads each wrote overlapping keys with no partial file and server accounting equal to the disk scan.
- **Not verified.** Bazel over HTTPS with a private certificate authority (the Bazel client trusts the JDK store, a public authority works), Windows and Linux, HTTP compression (unsupported in Bazel 8.4.2), a per-request log (a hook exists); the CI workflow still calls the old `cache remote status`.

### 🏎️ Performance objectives: `repo bench`

Speed is a requirement with numbers, and the numbers are a table. `repo bench` measures them in the disposable copy that `repo incremental prepare` provisions, judges them against one SLO table and compares with a stored baseline. The command is a staged set and must be applied after the resident service and the polyglot units. State: staged.

```bash
repo bench list
repo bench run --slo=probe:cli-help --runs=25
repo bench run --slo=kind:edit --runs=5 --save-baseline=<report>
repo bench run --prepare --slo=all --max-load=1 --baseline=<report> --markdown=<file>
repo bench compare --current=<report> --baseline=<report>
```

- **The user goals** are exact: a warm no-op `run` or `test` answers in at most 300 ms (resident p95 is blocking; the one-shot median is advisory, goal 300 ms and gate 1,000 ms), an edit to the start of the rebuild takes at most 500 ms (resident p95 per language is blocking, the one-shot median advisory) and a second run hits the cache 100 percent (minimum over runs, blocking, for a build and a test target). Further objectives: `repo --help` p95 goal 200 ms, a cold scope graph gate of 5 minutes, a warm scope graph, peak memory goal 512 MiB, and the longest wait of a concurrent agent for an unrelated project (goal 1 s, gate 5 s). 32 objectives with 62 rows (objective times implementation) exist; 8 objectives (16 rows) are blocking and the rest advisory, because nothing is blocking that cannot be reached today. Objectives without a goal and a gate (edit to green, makespans) only record and are guarded by the baseline.
- **21 probes** go through the copy's own `repo` CLI: `repo --help`; the warm no-op for a build and a test target, one-shot and through the resident service; the edit to the start of the rebuild and to green for four language units (TypeScript, Rust, Go, Python; each edits one source file with a private-body line and restores it byte for byte, also on failure and cancellation); the scope graph cold (once, only on a copy this run prepared) and warm; the second-run hit ratio (Bazel's compact execution log or the Nx run record; uncached tasks are not counted); three concurrent agents; and the peak memory of the process tree.
- **Statistics are honest.** n, minimum, p50, p95, p99, maximum and mean; percentiles interpolate linearly (the inclusive method of Python's `statistics.quantiles` and NumPy's default); a statistic needs enough samples (p50 and mean 3, p95 20, p99 100), otherwise the row is `unproven: too few samples`. Failed commands are counted and left out. The start of a rebuild counts only from a tool marker line; without one the value is a bound (first output = lower, process exit = upper) and the row stays unproven unless the bound already decides it.
- **Verdicts**: `pass`, `warn` (gate met, goal not), `fail`, `recorded`, `unproven` with a reason (`no samples`, `too few samples`, `probe failed`, `service unavailable`, `not cold`, `host saturated`, `start unobserved`, `unsupported`). Host load per core is annotated (quiet below 0.5, saturated from 1.5): a time that misses its gate on a saturated host is `unproven` because load only inflates it, a met goal stays met. `--max-load=<per core>` refuses a busy host.
- **Baseline and comparison.** Per objective and implementation: `improved`, `same`, `regressed`, `inconclusive` (a regression on a saturated host), `incomparable` (another host class, platform-arch-cores, or an unproven side), `new`, `missing`, with a tolerance per objective (a relative share plus an absolute margin). `--save-baseline=<file>` stores the report (the report format is the baseline format). Blocking objectives that fail or regress make the exit code fail after the reports were written.
- **Products and history.** The table holds the three products the build must keep fast (dashboard, architecture course, print) with five probes each: the first build on a fresh copy (`product-<p>-cold`, once, only on a copy this run prepared), the warm no-op, a one-line edit of the product source, and the build after a peer edit of an unrelated plugin script and of a scheduling file of the build library (kind `peer`: the file is edited, the product target is built, 0 re-executed actions are the budget); plus `product-agents`, three agents on the three products. Every sample records wall time, the peak memory of the process tree, the actions the tool re-executed, the projects the graph re-inferred and the wall time per phase (queues, graph preparation, tool execution), read line by line from the command output with the rules of `🧬️schema` (`phases`, `observation`; no line is kept). The budgets are the brief's targets: no-op under 10 s and 1.5 GiB, TypeScript edit to green under 60 s, dashboard edit under 2 min, peer edits 0 re-executions with graph preparation under 2 min and under 2 GiB. `--history=<folder>` stores every run as `<UTC start>-<host class>.json`, compares the next run with the newest earlier report of the host class, and `bench compare --history=<folder>` compares the two newest; a comparison also lists every blocking objective over its budget and fails for it.
- **Output**: text or `--json`, `--file=<report>`, `--markdown=<file>` (a GitHub step summary), English or German, progress per probe and sample (`bench:<probe>:<tool>:<i>/<n>`), cancellation before every command. CI gets a job `bench` on Linux and macOS with `continue-on-error: true` first; it stores a new baseline only after a push to the default branch.
- **Acceptance (`repo bench acceptance`).** The acceptance targets of the build system as a permanent, automated check on the checkout itself instead of a disposable copy: per product (course, print, dashboard) a hot no-op three times after two prime runs (the first builds, the second lets the graph memo settle), a peer-edit stage (comment lines appended to unrelated files and to a scheduling file of the build library when the first graph line appears, then restored; 0 re-executions), and a one-line edit with its byte-exact revert, all through the official `repo run` route with the exact wall clock of a monotonic clock and the physical footprint of the whole process tree sampled by the capacity governor's observer (macOS `phys_footprint`, Linux proportional set size, Windows private working set). The budgets are rows of `🛂️acceptance/🎯️budgets` (course hot no-op < 10 s and < 1.5 GB, course edit < 60 s, Rust edit < 120 s, dashboard no-op < 10 s, peer edits 0 re-executions; the dashboard no-op allows its one freshness worker, the dashboard edit and the print no-op time are advisory until a run holds them), each restating its bench objective by id where one exists. Verdicts are `pass`, `fail` or `unproven` with a reason (`clock grain`, `host saturated`, `resident only`, `not landed`, ...); a broken blocking budget, a failed run, an inexact restore, or a run that proved no blocking budget fails the command, after the JSON (`--file`) and markdown (`--markdown`) reports and the English or German text were written. `--in-place` is required for the scenarios that edit files (`all` leaves them out without it); edits are journaled under `.🧬semio/🦑️repo/⚡️cache/acceptance` and a killed run is repaired by the next one. The nightly job `.github/workflows/acceptance.yml` (schedule and manual dispatch, `macos-26`, optional larger runner through the variable `SEMIO_ACCEPTANCE_RUNNER`) runs it per product (the course row is blocking and fails on a breach; the dashboard and print rows are advisory until their first green nightly run) and uploads the JSON and markdown reports.
- **Open.** Until the resident service is applied the resident rows are `unproven (service unavailable)`; the start markers per tool must be calibrated on the first live edit run; the warm scope-graph gate of 2 s, the help p99 gate of 1 s and all tolerances are provisional; and the speed-up over the serial reference is collected but has no objective, because that reference follows the concurrent run and is warm.

### 🚫️ IDE-free: no editor configuration, plans belong to tickets

Agents never use an IDE, humans use the dashboard (`repo dashboard`), and no editor or IDE configuration registers or runs commands. The repository enforces that instead of asking for it. The following is a staged set that waits for the end of the cold graph build, because it touches the library and process trees. State: staged.

- **No client directories.** `.cursor` is forbidden outright, as is `.github/hooks`; `.windsurf` is classified as retired. The census law `ide-census` walks the physical tree and classifies the relative path, not only the entry name (it was blind to `.cursor` before), with the same verdicts in a TypeScript classifier and a SQLite oracle (7 new path cases; `.github/workflows` stays accepted). The dashboard authority and the Go formatter mirror the rule (`.cursor` no longer has a plans exception).
- **Plans are ticket files.** A plan is a file `<name>_<id>.plan.md` in its ticket folder, the convention `ticket_close` already uses for 111 archived plans. The 429 tracked Cursor plans and `.codex/plans/declarative-actions.md` leave the repository root: 15 move to the ticket whose `plan.id` names them, 75 to the ticket their own section or todo names (53 by rule, 22 curated), 1 stale duplicate is deleted, and the remaining 340, which have no ticket evidence, go into one closed archive ticket `ARCHIVED-CURSOR-PLANS-WITHOUT-TICKET` (title "Archived Cursor Plans Without Ticket", goal `AI-OPTIMIZED-REPO`). Seven tickets swap `plan.source` (an absolute client path, some from other machines) for `plan.local`. The `.gitignore` rule `!.🧬semio/🦑️repo/🎫️tickets/**/*.plan.md` keeps ticket plans trackable (two plans had been silently ignored). Cursor no longer carries a `plan_id` on any MCP surface; Kiro specs remain a plan source.
- **`editor` became `agent-client`.** Types, fixtures, directories and the hook output format that said "editor provider" now say "agent client" (a fail-closed codemod over an allowlist of 44 files; texts that mean a text editor or `.editorconfig` stay).
- **Leftovers.** The LSP plugins in `.claude/settings.json`, the empty `.github/hooks/repo.json`, the IDE rules of `.gitignore`, `"$schema"` editor hints in 20 tracked `tsconfig*.json` and the notebook dependencies in `pyproject.toml` are removed (`uv.lock` is regenerated with the Python lock command).
- **The VS Code extension is gone.** Agents never use an IDE, so the extension (59 tracked files, its manifest rows in six `package.json` files, the dependency-lock rows, the `vscode-test-configuration` validator and the `.vscodeignore` and `.vscode` metadata registrations) no longer exists; the history keeps the files.
- **Verified** on a copy: the census harness (3 tests, 206 expectations, a negative control that fails as it must), the dashboard authority fixture, Go vet on seven modules and the Go tests that exercise the change, 59 changed JSON files parse, the plan moves are reversible (`--undo`). Not run: the Rust edits (cargo is gated) and the library suites that need the installed dependencies.

### 🖥️ Cross-platform notes

| Host | Notes |
|---|---|
| macOS | Cache partitions are case-sensitive APFS images, no privilege. Keep the checkout path ASCII (Bazel crashes at startup on a non-ASCII install base and its sandbox mis-encodes a non-ASCII output base; the layout is refused up front). The adapter sets a UTF-8 locale. |
| Linux | Directory-backed partitions, no root and no loop device; needs a case-sensitive file system with atomic directory exchange (ext4, xfs, btrfs). A non-ASCII `install` path is refused; a non-ASCII output base works. In an unprivileged container Bazel falls back to `processwrapper-sandbox` (same results, weaker isolation); `linux-sandbox` needs a privileged container. The adapter never names a host-specific sandbox strategy: hermetic actions list `sandboxed`, which Bazel resolves to the best sandbox the host registers (`darwin-sandbox`, `linux-sandbox`, `processwrapper-sandbox`); naming `linux-sandbox` itself is refused by name in an unprivileged container. |
| Devcontainer | As Linux. `.cache` is a named volume private to the checkout (a bind mount from a case-insensitive host is refused by `repo preflight`); `postCreateCommand` runs the checked-in native shell bootstrap, which installs and verifies `repo` before `repo run workspace:setup`. |
| Windows | Directory partitions swapped by a journaled rename on NTFS with per-directory case sensitivity. Needs `LongPathsEnabled` and a short ASCII checkout root (the Bazel path budget is `len(output base) + len(\execroot\_main\s\) + 256` against 259, or 32766 with long paths). The source link is a junction (no developer mode, no elevation), runfiles are off (`--noenable_runfiles`, startup `--nowindows_enable_symlinks`), so tests run as cached actions instead of `bazel test`, and hermetic actions are remote-only. Set variables with `$env:NAME = "value"` in PowerShell and call `repo`. Not run on Windows. |

No cache path may exceed 256 characters on any host. Bazel and Bun are pinned per OS and CPU (darwin, linux, windows on arm64 and x64) with checksums, so `repo tools setup` needs no manual download.

### 🩹️ Troubleshooting

- **A command refuses after a reboot or image reattachment** ("Exact canonical persistent Native publication required …"). Publication receipts remain immutable. Device numbers are scoped to the current kernel namespace and can change after a reboot or a parent image reattachment within the same boot. A current publication is observed through the freshly anchored backing device while its published inode, mode, required ancestor chain, physical limits and kernel association remain exact; this does not rewrite a receipt or transfer deletion authority. An unsupported receipt is refused. The fixed **Reset Inactive Graph Cache** and **Reset Inactive Nx Cache** controls select only their declared inactive cache profile. Busy, foreign or incompletely observed ownership remains a diagnostic refusal (Recovery).
- **A command waits.** Stores are claimed through a first-in-first-out lease queue. A waiter at position 2 or later prints a `lease-queue` line every 15 seconds that names the holder's process id; a line `remains active on …` is progress of a running walk, not a wait. Do not kill a holder that is making progress; read the head ticket first. A line that starts with `[capacity]` comes from the host capacity governor, not from a lease: it names the reason and your queue position (Host capacity); `repo leases status` lists who holds what and who waits for whom. A dependency sync or a cold graph build holds its image or its facts lease for a long time (Leases, lock order and queues).
- **A receipt fails.** Store receipt: one task re-runs uncached (`[store-receipt] re-executing`). Dependency generation receipt: an edit of any file in its closure costs the next command one complete walk (progress `dependencies:generation-verify:<n>`). Image receipt: see the first item.
- **No cache hit.** Check in this order: `--skip-cache` or the dashboard axis `cache` set to a skip value; the target's decision in the manifest (`unclassified` never replays); an undeclared or too wide input; the mode (`prototype` vs `release`); another process editing declared inputs during the build; for a remote hit a different user or checkout path.
- **Remote cache.** `repo remote-cache status` shows `configured: false` when no endpoint is set, `reachable: false` when the endpoint does not answer and `authorized: false` for a wrong token (Nx aborts a run on 401, ignores 403 on upload silently).
- **Workers.** Rerun with `--workers off` to separate a worker problem from a task problem. A build that fails with "Captured implementation Source changed" saw a build-tool file change under it; rerun it.
- **Installed packages.** `Cannot find module` for a workspace package means the dependencies are older than the sources: `repo dependencies lock --all`, then `repo dependencies sync --all`. On a fresh clone the Nx setup may need the installed workspace links: if it stops with `Cannot find module`, run the sync first.
- **Nx reports `.nx/installation` missing.** Run `repo tools setup nx`.

### ✅️ Verified and not yet verified

Verified on 2026-10-20 (macOS arm64, Bazel 8.4.2, Nx 23.2.0; Linux arm64 container for the platform rows): real-Bazel cold, warm, after-`clean` and after-server-loss runs with the production task owner, early cutoff, tests as cached `bazel test`, `affected` against Bazel `rdeps` and Nx's locators, real Bazel and a pristine Nx against `repo cache serve` (remote hits after clearing the local cache, wrong and read-only tokens), persistent workers against real Bazel, the hermetic law, the Linux container setup, and the fixture suites of every component. Not yet verified: a production `repo --tool=bazel run` after the integration, Nx with the repository's patched install against a remote cache, a real remote executor and remote execution of any repository target, Windows, the devcontainer lifecycle, and every end-to-end chain under both tools (see Verification status).

Verified in copies of the tree on 2026-10-20, not yet through `repo` on the live checkout: the lock order with real child processes (deadlock victims, starvation, a wait-for graph against a SQLite oracle), Bazel server slots with real Bazel 8.4.2, Cargo lanes and the unit cache with real Cargo and sccache as oracle, graph generations against an independent Python model, the Nx graph receipt and concurrent writers against stock Nx 23.2.0, concurrent dependency generations with real Bun, the cache ledger against a SQLite oracle, TypeScript check units against `tsc -p`, the host capacity decision against an independent SQLite implementation, the incremental and concurrency judges (29 and 26 tests, with real Bazel), `repo verify build-parity` with fixtures and a fake `repo`, the boot sweep with real `hdiutil` and SIGKILL injection, and the test sandbox (16 tests; its law finds the same 1,042 findings in TypeScript and in a separate Python reference). Not yet verified: a production `repo --tool=bazel run`, a cold `repo show projects` on the new graph code, `repo incremental prepare` and its first judged scenarios, the first `repo concurrency run`, `repo verify build-parity` against the real tools, every staged set on the live tree, and Linux, Windows and the devcontainer for all of them.

## 🧭️ Neutral model

| Layer | What it is | Source |
|---|---|---|
| Manifest | One `📋️project.json` per project: targets with executor, command, inputs, outputs, `cache`, `continuous`, `dependsOn`. | schema `📚️library/🏗️build/🧬️schema/📋️project/🔣️.json` |
| Policy | `defaultTool`, the named input groups (`default`, `production`, `sharedGlobals`, `cargo`, `go`, `uv`, `dotnet`, `schemaSources`, `nativeSources`, `nativeTestSources`) and the files that may change the graph. | `📚️library/🏗️build/🔣️policy.json` |
| Graph | `BuildGraphV1`: projects, targets and dependency edges inferred in TypeScript from the manifests, Cargo, Go, `package.json` and `bun.lock`; independent of Nx. Snapshots live in the graph partition `g`. `show` and `graph` print it. | `📚️library/🏗️build/🕸️graph` |
| Operations | The catalogue of operations, which tool implements each one how, and the JSON shapes their results must have. | `📚️library/🏗️build/⚙️operations/🔣️catalog.json`, schema `⚙️operations/🧬️schema/🔣️.json` |
| Implementations | Nx adapter and Bazel adapter; each one only translates the neutral request into its tool. | Sections Nx implementation and Bazel implementation below. |

**Tool selection.** `--tool=nx|bazel` on the command line wins, then the environment variable `SEMIO_BUILD_TOOL`, then `defaultTool` of the policy (`nx`). The selected tool is exported as `SEMIO_BUILD_TOOL` to every nested task, so a task that calls `repo` again keeps the tool; the dashboard axis `build-tool` sets the same variable (see Command registry). Anything else is refused ("Unknown build tool"). Choose the compiler configuration with `--configuration=prototype|release` (or `-c`, a `:<configuration>` suffix on the task, or `SEMIO_BUILD_MODE`); disagreeing values are refused ("Conflicting build modes", stated in English and German, naming the fixed-mode targets such as `dev` that pin a mode). The mode is one invocation axis, not a per-target setting: the Bazel implementation accepts `prototype` and `release` for every target whether or not the target declares them as configurations, every planned task of every target carries the mode in its id, label and workspace, the dependencies of the selected task inherit the mode, the original caller's mode is the authority every task environment exports (`SEMIO_BUILD_MODE`) and is part of every action key, and the plan refuses a task whose mode differs from the caller's and a dependency whose target is fixed to another mode. Only `prototype` and `release` can be selected at the command line; other configuration names exist only in the Bazel adapter. The course verbs that need release (`publish`, `test-e2e` rehearsal, `docker-image-build`, `docker-image-publish`, `deploy-check` of `@teaching/architecture-course`) read the original caller's mode and refuse a prototype caller in English and German; select release with `repo [--tool=bazel] run @teaching/architecture-course:publish:release`.

For an exact `run <canonical-project-name>:<target>` selection, `repo` reads `📋️build-owners.json` and that owner’s JSON manifest before issuing the original compiler mode. A target may declare `metadata.semio.buildMode.fixed` as `prototype` or `release`; this is a constraint and conflicting selections are refused. `defaultConfiguration` is a fallback used when no mode was explicitly selected or inherited. Named fixed workflows keep their mode. Unknown owners, inferred targets and Cargo manifests add no authored mode. `run-many` and `affected` retain backend-owned project selectors, exclusions and changed-file filtering; they use the explicitly selected or inherited mode, named workflow mode, or the generic prototype fallback without reading owner declarations.

**Operations.** The state of each operation per tool: `native` = the tool's own command, `adapter` = our code drives the tool, `neutral` = tool independent, `missing` = not implemented and the call refuses.

| Operation | Nx | Bazel | `--dry-run` | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `run <project:target[:configuration]>` | native | adapter | yes | A dry run executes nothing. |
| `run-many --targets=… [--projects=…]` | native | adapter | yes | |
| `affected --targets=…` | native | adapter | yes | Changed files are the tracked changes against `HEAD` plus untracked files; `--base`/`--head`, `--files`, `--uncommitted`, `--untracked` narrow them and `--all` selects every project. Affected projects (the same rule for both tools, asserted against Nx's own locators): the deepest project root that owns a changed file, projects whose target inputs name a changed `{workspaceRoot}/…` file, every project when `nx.json` changes, then all dependents. `--projects` is refused for every tool; use `run-many` to narrow. |
| `exec --projects=… -- <command>` | native | adapter | yes | Once per selected project, working directory is the project root, and the tool-neutral pair `SEMIO_PROJECT_NAME` (project name) and `SEMIO_PROJECT_ROOT` (project root relative to the workspace) is exported by both tools; the Nx implementation maps the names its own `exec` exports (see Nx implementation). Under Bazel each project gets an uncached `repo-exec` task. `--dry-run` prints the `repo-exec` plan and runs nothing. |
| `watch --projects=… -- <command>` | neutral | neutral | no | Reruns the command when selected source files change. |
| `graph`, `show projects\|project <name>\|targets` | neutral | neutral | no | Reads the portable graph; never starts Nx or Bazel, by design. |
| `reset` | adapter | adapter | no | Nx: invalidates the graph and runs `nx reset`. Bazel: invalidates the graph and empties `bazel/actions` (the disk cache), `bazel/outputs` and `bazel/workspaces` of the products partition. |
| `tools setup [nx\|bazel\|toolchains\|all]`, `tools verify [tool...]` | adapter | adapter | no | Without an argument the selected tool is set up (`--tool`, default `nx`). See Setup per platform below. `tools setup bazel` needs neither the JavaScript install nor Nx and opens only the tools and dependencies partitions; the products partition is opened by the first run. |
| `preflight` | neutral | neutral | no | Checks the host before any store is provisioned, from `📚️library/🏗️build/🩺️preflight/🔣️policy.json`: CPU architecture, 8 GiB free, required tools (`cc` and `pkg-config` on Linux, `cc` and `hdiutil` on macOS) and, on Linux, atomic directory exchange (`renameat2` `RENAME_EXCHANGE`) on a case-sensitive workspace file system. |
| `dependencies sync\|lock --all\|--scope <owner>` | neutral | neutral | no | Bun installs the JavaScript dependencies for both tools, by design. |
| `cargo …` | neutral | neutral | no | The repository's compile ingress; Bazel's Cargo actions call it, so it is the leaf of both tools, by design. Run Rust targets with `run <project>:build\|check\|test` to use Bazel's cache. |
| `cache status\|gc\|clear` | adapter | adapter | no | Nx: the artifact directory of the rust partition. Bazel: the disk cache of the products partition. See the two implementation sections. The remote cache has its own operation `remote-cache` (see Remote cache). |

**Flags.** Flags before `--` are portable and validated before any tool starts; an unknown flag, a stray positional argument or an invalid selector is refused. Everything after `--` is passed literally to the task. The flag list is part of the catalogue and is printed by `repo --help`; the task commands (`run`, `run-many`, `affected`, `exec`, `watch`) accept all flags of the table, but a flag only has an effect where the last column names its command.

| Flag | Meaning | Commands |
|---|---|---|
| `--tool=nx\|bazel` | Select the implementation (otherwise `SEMIO_BUILD_TOOL`, then the policy default). | all |
| `--workers=on\|off` | Run actions in persistent workers (`on`) or one process each (`off`); otherwise `SEMIO_BUILD_WORKERS`, then the worker policy. | all |
| `--help`, `-h`; `--lang=en\|de` | Print the usage of the command in English or German and stop. | all |
| `--projects`, `-p`, `--exclude` | Comma lists of project names with `*` wildcards, `tag:<tag>`, `name:<name>`, `directory:<root>`, `!<pattern>` negation. | `run-many`, `exec`, `watch`; `--exclude` also `affected` |
| `--targets`, `--target`, `-t` | Target names or `*` patterns. | `run-many`, `affected` |
| `--configuration`, `-c` | Compiler configuration (`cargo` takes the full name only). | `run`, `run-many`, `affected`, `cargo` |
| `--parallel=<n>`, `--bail`, `--verbose`, `--output=stream\|stream-without-prefixes\|static` (alias `--output-style`) | Concurrency, stop at first failure, detail, output style. | `run`, `run-many`, `affected` |
| `--skip-cache`, `--skip-remote-cache`, `--exclude-dependencies` (alias `--exclude-task-dependencies`) | Ignore the task cache, ignore the remote cache, run only the named tasks. | `run`, `run-many`, `affected` |
| `--dry-run`, `--graph[=<file>]` | Print the task graph as a `DryRunPlan` on stdout and execute nothing. `--graph` is the same flag on both tools; with a file the plan is written there (Nx: the file as given, Bazel: a report whose path is printed). | `run`, `run-many`, `affected`, `exec` |
| `--all`, `--base`, `--head`, `--files`, `--uncommitted`, `--untracked` | Change selection (`--all`: every project). | `affected`; `--all` also `watch` and `dependencies` |
| `--include-global-files` | `watch` also reacts to files outside any project. | `watch` |
| `--file=<path>`, `--json` | Write the result as a report file; print lists as JSON. | `graph`, `show` |
| `--scope <owner>` | Restrict the operation to one dependency owner (instead of `--all`). | `dependencies` |
| `--store=shared\|isolated`, `--parallelism=<cores>` | Cargo target store and core count. | `cargo` |
| `--port`, `--host`, `--directory`, `--max-bytes` | Port, address, store directory and size bound of the remote cache server (`--name value` or `--name=value`); `--read-only`, `--tls-certificate` and `--tls-key` switch it to read-only or HTTPS. | `remote-cache` |

**Result shapes.** Results that scripts consume are specified by the schema `⚙️operations/🧬️schema/🔣️.json`:

- `DryRunPlan`: `{"tasks":{"roots":[<id>],"tasks":{<id>:{"id":…,"target":{"project":…,"target":…,"configuration"?:…}}},"dependencies":{<id>:[<id>]},"continuousDependencies"?:{<id>:[<id>]}}}`. A task id is `<project>:<target>[:<configuration>]`; `exec` uses the target `repo-exec`. The Bazel plan adds `selected` (the requested task ids) and lists dependencies on continuous tasks apart in `continuousDependencies`, because those only have to be running.
- `CacheStatus`: `{"version":1,"tool","available","directory","entries","bytes","activity"?:{"builds","actions","executed","cacheHits"}}` (`activity` is filled by Bazel). `CacheClear`: `{"version":1,"tool","cleared","removedEntries","removedBytes"}`.

## 📋️ Manifests

A task in `targets` of a `📋️project.json` uses these fields:

| Field | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `executor` | `repo:script` (default, runs `command` through the repo process authority) or `repo:noop` (ordering only, runs nothing). |
| `command`, `options.command`, `options.commands`, `options.args` | What runs; `args` are literal arguments that are never shell-interpolated. `configurations.<name>` may override `command`, `commands`, `cwd`, `env`, `args`. `defaultConfiguration` names the configuration used when none is requested. |
| `dependsOn` | Tasks that must finish first: `"target"` (same project), `"^target"` (dependency projects), `"project:target"`, or `{target, projects, dependencies, params}`. |
| `inputs` | What the result depends on, see below. |
| `outputs` | Paths the task writes, relative tokens `{projectRoot}`, `{workspaceRoot}` and option tokens such as `{options.env.SEMIO_BUILD_MODE}`. `[]` means "no files to restore". |
| `cache` | Boolean, see the classes below. |
| `continuous` | The task is a long-running service. |
| `metadata.semio.dashboard.ready` | How readiness of a continuous task is detected: the output shows `http://(127.0.0.1\|localhost\|0.0.0.0):<port>` and that address answers 2xx or 3xx; `port` or `portEnv` declares the port, `path` appends a path, `printed` takes the whole printed address. |

**Inputs.** A list of file globs (`{projectRoot}/**/*`, `{workspaceRoot}/…`, `!` excludes), named groups from the policy (`default`, `production`, `cargo`, …), `^<group>` (the same group of the dependency projects), and objects: `{"env": "NAME"}`, `{"runtime": "<command>"}` (the command's output), `{"externalDependencies": [...]}` and `{"dependentTasksOutputFiles": "<glob>", "transitive": true|false}` (the outputs of the tasks in `dependsOn`). Declare everything the task reads that is not reachable from its own project: data outside the project, spawned test files, tool and toolchain files, environment. A project can add its own `namedInputs` (for example `reads`, `testEnv`, `cargoToolchain`). `default` of a project replaces the workspace `default`, so re-add `sharedGlobals` (platform, Bun version, `SEMIO_BUILD_MODE`) there.

**Command source closure.** A `repo:script` target needs no hand-written list of the modules its script imports. The graph inference derives the closure of the command script (`📚️library/🏗️build/🕸️graph/📥️inference/🧭️script-inputs`): relative imports, imports through workspace package specifiers such as `@semio-tech/process/leases` (`workspacePackageModuleV1`; installed registry packages stay covered by their locked external dependency), literal `new URL("…", import.meta.url)` targets that exist (modules are followed, other files are hashed as resources), and literal module inputs a target declares (for example `{projectRoot}/…/x.test.ts`). The closure joins the target as named inputs `commandSources…`; in the hash representation of the graph it is the marker `{"env": "SEMIO_SOURCE_CLOSURE_<sha256 of the manifest>"}` and the manifest sits in the graph store under `build-input-closures`, which Nx reads when it hashes the task. Cargo lock projections are markers of the same kind (`SEMIO_CARGO_LOCK_<key>`). Bazel expands the markers itself (see Bazel implementation). A target opts into more derived closure with `metadata.semio.closure`: `modules` lists `{projectRoot}`/`{workspaceRoot}` globs of module files (a test directory the router never imports) whose matches join as entries together with their import closure, and `typescript` lists tsconfig paths whose compiler program (include set, path mappings, `.d.ts` siblings, type-only imports, `extends` chain) joins the closure and names the installed type packages it resolved. References such as `new URL("x.json", origin(import.meta.url))` and `join`/`resolve(<module directory>, "x.json")` with literal segments join as files. Deliberately not covered: in-source test imports guarded by `import.meta.vitest`, files read through computed paths, and targets that are never cached (continuous, live). The course keeps this honest with `🏗️build/🕸️graph/📥️inference/🧪️tests/🎓️course-inputs` (esbuild, the TypeScript program and an independent Python implementation against the manifest). Measured on a sample of 49 project scripts the closure grew from a median of 489 to 1,200 files (about 28 percent more CPU for the derivation, offset by an admission memo that cut the old cost by 35 percent). Not yet verified: the assembled inputs in a real graph load (`repo show projects` was stopped on 2026-10-20 after 4 hours; the later analysis found it garbage-collection bound under host memory starvation, not quadratic in the closure code: a project costs 0.3 to 0.45 CPU-s, the closure derivation takes about 29 percent of that, and a clean run is estimated at 25 to 40 minutes), an Nx hash of a real task, and a Bazel run with expanded closures against the production entry.

**Cache classes.** `cache` is the switch both tools read. Next to it the inference attaches a machine-readable decision `metadata.semio.cache = {"version":1,"class":…,"reason":…}` to every target it forces, synthesizes or refuses to replay (closed schema `📚️library/🏗️build/🏃️execution/🧬️schema/💾️cache.json`). The class names the physical fact that allows or forbids a replay:

| Class | Reasons | Meaning | Cached |
|---|---|---|---|
| `deterministic` | `pure-function-of-inputs`, `check-without-outputs` | A pure function of its declared inputs; every declared output is a plain restorable tree, or there is none. | yes, the only replayable class |
| `driver-owned` | `freshness-owned-by-driver` | The command compiles through the build driver, whose Cargo freshness and stage cache are the authority (playground `prepare-*` and `build-*-react*`, `component`). | no |
| `managed-output` | `outputs-are-store-links`, `outputs-in-managed-store` | Outputs live in the leased products store behind a binding or manifest protocol (TypeScript artifact `build`, wasm slots) and the manifest does not author `cache: true`; restoring a copy would be refused or bypass the store. With `cache: true` the target is `deterministic` instead, because its receipt is the restorable output (see Store receipts). | no |
| `continuous` | `serves-until-stopped` | Runs until stopped. | no |
| `side-effecting` | `publishes-live-state`, `publishes-remote`, `mutates-workspace`, `mutates-host-store` | Mutates state outside its declared outputs. | no |
| `live` | `drives-running-process`, `exercises-host-resources` | Observes or drives a running process (names with `e2e` or `live`), or exercises the real locks, storage, process tree or clock of this machine. | no |
| `aggregate` | `orders-prerequisites`, `reenters-build-tool` | Orders prerequisites and does no work of its own. | no |
| `diagnostic` | `admission-refusal`, `measures-environment`, `reads-caller-input` | Reports a refusal or a measurement and is always re-evaluated. | no |
| `unclassified` | `no-cache-classification` | No row, declaration or verifying command classifies the target, so it never replays until the policy names its kind (fail-closed). | no |

The Bazel adapter reads the decision (`bazelTargetCacheClassV1` validates it against the closed schema and refuses an invalid one with "Closed repository cache decision required") and derives its cache decision from the class, see the Bazel cache decision below. The Nx projection does not read it; Nx decides from `cache`, `continuous` and `outputs`. A manifest may declare it itself (a target with `cache: false` records its reason this way, see the Build tools guide); the synthesized playground targets, the TypeScript artifact `test`, `check` and (with `cache: true`) `build` targets, and the wasm slot producers with `cache: true` carry `deterministic`, targets cached by the name rules below carry the class of their row. The policy that sets `cache` and the decision follows this order, the first matching row wins (`📚️library/🏗️build/🕸️graph/📥️inference/🎯️target-policy`, names from `📚️library/⚡️caching/🔣️policy.json`):

| Order | Rule | Cached | Decision attached |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | `continuous: true`, or a name `dev`, `serve`, `watch`, `start`, `test-watch`, `daemon` (also `<name>-…`) | no | `continuous` |
| 2 | `cache: false` in the manifest | no | none |
| 3 | A name of the `uncached` list (`audit`, `bench`, `clean`, `deploy`, `deps`, `install`, `publish`, `seed`, `setup`, `test-e2e`, `update`, …) or an exact name (`format`, `commit`, `os`, `parity`, …); `cache: true` is overridden | no | `live` (`e2e`, `live`, `os`, `semio`), `diagnostic` (`audit`, `bench`, `doctor`, `parity`, …), `side-effecting` (`publish`, `deploy` as `publishes-remote`, otherwise `mutates-workspace`) |
| 4 | `hub-live-catalog-check`, `hub-live-catalog-native-check`, `hub-live-catalog-oracle` | yes | none (exceptions to row 5) |
| 5 | A dash-separated word `clean`, `gc`, `prune`, `setup`, `fuzz`, `e2e` or `live` in the name | no | as row 3 |
| 6 | The decision the target declares in `metadata.semio.cache`, else the first matching row of `targetClasses` in `📚️library/⚡️caching/🔣️policy.json` (ordered rows of exact names, heads, tails and tokens, each with a decision; an exact name beats the rest): `deterministic/check-without-outputs` for the heads `test`, `check`, `lint`, `typecheck`, `format-check`, `verify`, `schema`, `stdio`, a trailing `-check`, `-test`, `-oracle`, `-lint`, `-source` or `-native`, the token `contract` and a list of exact names; `deterministic/pure-function-of-inputs` for the heads `build` and `generate` and a list of exact producers; `side-effecting/mutates-workspace` for the head `generator` | yes when the class is `deterministic`, otherwise no | the class of the row; for `deterministic` the default inputs `default` and `^default`, outputs `[]` unless the manifest says otherwise |
| 7 | A `verify` command in the target | yes | `deterministic/check-without-outputs` |
| 8 | Anything else | **no** | `unclassified/no-cache-classification` |

Row 8 is fail-closed under both tools: a target that nothing classifies runs every time, whatever its `cache` field says (`unclassified`); the cache-correctness law (`📚️library/⚖️laws/🔒️cache-correctness`) reports it together with every `cache: true` that the policy overrode and every `cache: false` without a recorded reason. A `deterministic/pure-function-of-inputs` target that declares no output is demoted to `side-effecting/mutates-workspace` before either tool sees it (`cachedOutputsAdmittedV1`), because a hit would replay an empty success. Bazel additionally requires an `outputs` array and a class that allows replay, see its cache decision. Write `cache: false` plus a decision for these reasons (the convention across the manifests, mirrored by the classes above): tests that exercise real storage, lease, process or toolchain state; live host, browser or server runs; writers that rewrite the tree or a store outside their declared outputs; launchers of long-lived tools; network downloads; steps whose input set is unbounded (digests of the whole workspace).

**Store receipts.** Products that are published into a leased, content-addressed store instead of the project directory (Cargo artifacts in the compiler store, Vite sites, wasm packages, TypeScript artifacts) cannot be restored by copying files into that store from outside. They declare a receipt as their only output, `{projectRoot}/.🧾️store/<target>.json` (schema `📚️library/🏗️build/🧾️store-receipt/🧬️schema/🔣️.json`, git-ignored, deliberately not below `dist`, which is a link into the store for TypeScript artifacts). The receipt names each published store path (`entries`: kind, bytes, files, SHA-256; a directory digest hashes its sorted file list) and each link that binds a project path to a store path (`links`). The central publishers write it after publication, so no build script changes: the artifact destination that the Vite and wasm publishers write through (`⚡️caching/📦️artifacts/🟦️.ts`), the compiler store publisher of Cargo `build` (`⚡️caching/📦️artifacts/🏗️native-build/🟦️.ts`, skipped when an observation output is requested) and the TypeScript artifact binder (`⚡️caching/📦️artifacts/🟦️typescript/🔗️binding/🟦️.ts`). Both adapters hand the receipt location to the one task that declares it (`SEMIO_TASK_RECEIPT`, issued by `issueStoreReceiptV1` in `📚️library/🏗️build/🧾️store-receipt/🟨️.mjs`; Nx: `⚡️caching/🚀️bootstrap/🔌️nx-plugin/📤️arguments/🟨️.mjs`, Bazel: `executeTask` in `📚️library/🏗️build/🔧️tools/🏗️bazel/📜️script.ts`); every other task has it unset, so a nested task never records into its parent's receipt, and a publisher does nothing without it (`recordStoreContentV1`).

After every `run` or `run-many`, both tools verify the receipt of each selected task and of its prerequisites. A receipt that is absent, damaged or points at a missing or changed store entry counts as a miss: that one task reruns alone and uncached (Nx: `run <task> --skip-nx-cache --excludeTaskDependencies`, the one extra child selection the caller admits, which also replaces the cache entry; Bazel: in-process with `skipCache` and without dependencies, which leaves the stale disk-cache entry in place, so a later hit repairs again) and is verified again, and the invocation fails closed otherwise. A failed first run is retried once after the repair. A task that declares a receipt but whose handler wrote none is an error, not silently accepted. TypeScript artifact `build` and wasm slot producers carry the receipt as their output: they replay when the manifest authors `cache: true` (decision `deterministic`) and stay `managed-output` otherwise. A target declares a receipt only when its `outputs` list the receipt path (`declaredStoreReceiptV1`): `check`, `test` and `lint` publish a verdict, not store content, and `publish` and the course `build` list other outputs or none. Alternating `prototype` and `release` runs of one target share one receipt file (its path has no mode), so a switch is expected to rerun the target once (inferred from the path rule, not observed). Not yet verified: no run has exercised the repair path (first proof: run `@teaching/proctor:build` twice, delete the compiler `published/<hash>` slot, run again and expect one `[store-receipt] re-executing` line, with `--tool=nx` and `--tool=bazel`).

The Bazel implementation decides per action from `cache`, `continuous` and `outputs` and is stricter about missing declarations (see its cache decision below).

## 🔌️ Nx implementation

Nx is the default (`defaultTool`). The repository does not use Nx project discovery; it feeds the neutral graph into Nx.

- **Plugin.** `nx.json` registers the plugin `@repo/emoji-project-json` (`📚️library/🟨️.mjs`) for `📋️project.json`. Its `createNodesV2` publishes the neutral graph as Nx projects and its `createDependencies` publishes the neutral edges; it refuses edges whose projects it does not know. Each target is projected onto an Nx executor and receives a private engine identity as an additional input, so a change of the executing implementation invalidates cached results. That identity is ONE runtime input, a SHA-256 over the current bytes of the executor's import closure (`⚡️caching/🚀️bootstrap/🔌️nx-plugin/🧮️engine-digest`, run once per Nx client), not the list of its files: a task plan stays small however large the closure grows.
- **Executors.** `@semio-tech/repo-lib:command` and `@semio-tech/repo-lib:owner-command` (`📚️library/📦️packages/🟦️typescript/📋️executors.json`) `command` runs the literal command arguments through the original process authority and the Bun shell, `owner-command` forwards the literal argv to the native or process owner without shell interpolation (descriptions in `executors.json`); `repo:noop` becomes `nx:noop`.
- **Translation.** `--skip-cache` becomes `--skip-nx-cache`, `--exclude-dependencies` becomes `--excludeTaskDependencies`, `--output` becomes `--output-style`, `--bail` becomes `--nx-bail`, `--dry-run` and a bare `--graph` (or `--graph=true`) become `--graph=stdout`, `--graph=<file>` is kept. `exec` runs the command in a Bun wrapper that copies the names Nx's own `exec` exports (`NX_PROJECT_NAME`, `NX_PROJECT_ROOT_PATH`) to the neutral `SEMIO_PROJECT_NAME` and `SEMIO_PROJECT_ROOT`; the Nx names exist only in this implementation. `affected` without a selector adds `--base=HEAD`, `--uncommitted --untracked` becomes `--base=HEAD`, `--all` becomes `run-many`. `exec --dry-run` prints the neutral `repo-exec` plan and runs nothing.
- **Boundary.** Production code outside `⚡️caching/🚀️bootstrap` never loads an Nx module. It goes through the typed port `⚡️caching/🚀️bootstrap/🛠️tools/🔌️sdk`, whose closed catalogue names the 13 Nx modules the repository may touch. A test scans every production file under `🦑️repo/🔨️modules` with an own tokenizer and the TypeScript compiler as an independent oracle and requires zero offenders.
- **Cache location.** Nx task results live in `.🧬semio/🦑️repo/⚡️cache/rust/fixed/m/nx/artifacts` (`cacheDirectory` in `nx.json`; the rust partition, 48 GiB, which also holds the shared Cargo target and build directories of `.cargo/config.toml`). The separate partition `n` (`.cache/b/n`, 1.5 GiB) is the Nx pool: it holds the Nx graph cache as funded child images (`⚡️caching/💾️storage/🔒️enforcement/🔌️nx/📦️graph`), not the task results. `repo cache status` walks the artifact directory and prints `CacheStatus`; `cache clear` runs `nx reset --onlyCache` inside the bounded namespace and prints `CacheClear`.
- **Not yet verified.** The whole flow (a real cold run that fills `nx/artifacts`, then a warm run served from it) is scripted but has not been run at the time of writing; the unit and contract tests of the pieces above passed on 2026-10-19 (`⚡️caching/🚀️bootstrap/🛠️tools/⚙️operations`, `🔌️sdk`, `🔁️probe`, `🏗️build/🧭️selection`, `🏗️build/🧭️invocation`: 23 tests, 0 failures). On 2026-10-20 the link `.nx/installation` pointed to a tree under `.cache/b/t/m/nx` that no longer exists (only `.stage-copy-*` leftovers), so every Nx call needs `repo tools setup nx` first.

## 🏗️ Bazel implementation

`--tool=bazel` (or `SEMIO_BUILD_TOOL=bazel`) selects Bazel 8.4.2 (`.bazelversion`, pinned download per OS and CPU). The adapter is `📚️library/🏗️build/🔧️tools/🏗️bazel`. It turns the selected tasks into real Bazel actions, so Bazel's own action cache, disk cache, optional remote cache and lazy evaluation do the work.

- **Generated workspace.** For each distinct selection the adapter writes a workspace under `<products>/bazel/workspaces/<namespace>`: `MODULE.bazel`, a `BUILD.bazel` file, the rules in `tasks.bzl` (`repo_task`, `repo_launch`, `repo_noop`), a task manifest `<label>.json` and a fingerprint `<label>.input` per action, and `s`, a link to the real checkout (a junction on Windows). A task label is `t_` plus 16 hex digits of the SHA-256 of the task id.
- **Minimal builds.** Only the selected tasks and the tasks reachable through `dependsOn` become actions, and the build is `bazel build` of exactly the selected labels; pure ordering tasks (`repo:noop`) vanish behind their producers unless they are selected themselves. A target that is not needed is never analyzed or run. A selected cached `test` task without declared outputs and without selected dependents runs as a Bazel test with cached test results (see Task dispositions in the Build tools guide).
- **Declared inputs.** The `inputs` of a cacheable task (named groups, `^` expansion, globs, `!` exclusions, `externalDependencies` as `bun.lock` and `package.json`, `dependentTasksOutputFiles` as the producers' outputs) are resolved against the files of the checkout and declared as Bazel inputs: the files reach the action through shared `filegroup`s of labels `s/<path>` instead of one list per task: a directory whose files are all selected is one group `d_<hash>` (a project root is one group, single-child chains collapse), pattern units with more than two members are shared composites `k_<hash>`, and each task gets one composite `i_<hash>` (`bazelSourceCoverV1`; names are ASCII, emoji appear only in file labels). On the static graph of 1,466 projects this cut `BUILD.bazel` from 317 MB to 65 MB and workspace generation from 351 s to 37 s. `glob()` was tried and rejected: with `.bazelignore` it matches nothing, and without it follows symlink loops and git-ignored trees that Nx never hashes. `^name` and `dependencies: true` reach the transitive dependency closure, as Nx hashes them (`bazelTaskInputsV1`); a `dependsOn` that names an absent project or target is refused when the plan is built (`requireBazelDependencyV1`, "Unknown build task dependency") and `bazelDanglingDependenciesV1` lists all of them at once. Paths Bazel cannot name as labels (label-unsafe names, directories, dangling links, anything below a nested `BUILD` package) are hashed into the fingerprint instead. The fingerprint also carries the host platform and CPU, the Bun version, the build mode, declared environment values and `runtime` command outputs. The action manifest is content-stable: it names the task and the executing implementation but no workspace, runtime or snapshot path; the live data sits in `runtime.json`, outside every key.
- **Command closures.** Closure markers (`SEMIO_SOURCE_CLOSURE_<key>`, `SEMIO_CARGO_LOCK_<key>`) are expanded to the files they name before a declaration or a fingerprint is built (`📚️library/🏗️build/🔧️tools/🏗️bazel/🔗️closure`): `bazelClosureMarkersForPlanV1` collects the markers a plan can reach (task inputs, `^` edges and `{input, projects}` references), the manifests are fetched by content address (`managedBazelClosureFetchV1` through the graph store reader of the Nx hasher; a filesystem variant for tests), validated against a closed schema, and names that are absolute or contain `..` are refused. A marker met without loaded manifests is an error, not an empty list. Before this, no closure file reached a Bazel action key, so editing an imported module left a task cached (measured on 2026-10-20 in a scratch run: 106 of 111 modules of one script and 162 of 169 of another were undeclared).
